    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/faith-evidence-ie/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — StructFuse\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main\\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings\\n- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026\\n\\n## Core Claims (3)\\n\\n1. **Structure-grounded hard negatives outperform embedding-distance and random negatives for contrastive code retrieval training.** Inter-procedural data-flow connectivity provides a complementary training signal that enhances text-based retrieval — files that are lexically similar but structurally disconnected force the model to internalize structural relevance beyond surface features. (Note: structural signal is complementary but subordinate to text similarity; abstract must reflect this asymmetry per Dream review.)\\n\\n2. **Embedding-distance hard negatives actively harm code retrieval compared to random negatives.** The gradient signal from embedding-distance negatives is partially redundant with the encoder's learned similarity, leading to degraded retrieval quality. (MVP evidence: -27pp recall@1 at pool=26, 3 seeds consistent.)\\n\\n3. **Scope-bounded inter-procedural data-flow analysis via CodeQL is a scalable, model-independent method for computing structural connectivity.** With 2-hop call-graph bounding, timeout <5%, and >40% of instances have connected-but-not-gold structural signals at scale.\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Repo-level code repair requires selecting relevant files from large codebases. Current retrievers conflate lexical similarity with structural relevance — a file sharing function names with the buggy code may be structurally unrelated, while a data-flow-connected file may contain the actual fix dependency.\\n\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**:\\n  - Text-based (SweRank, SWE-Fixer): blind to structural dependencies\\n  - Graph-guided (LocAgent, RANGER, CodexGraph): structural graphs at inference only, no data-flow edges, no training signal\\n  - ARISE: data-flow but intra-procedural only (no call boundary crossing), runtime tool calls only\\n  - CoCoHaNeRe: hard negatives improve code search, but embedding-distance negatives are redundant with encoder similarity\\n\\n- **Our framing**: The key gap is using structural connectivity for **training signal**, not just inference-time navigation. Structure-grounded hard negatives — lexically similar but data-flow disconnected files — provide a complementary learning signal. This is a general paradigm: any domain with external structural knowledge (molecular networks, circuit topology, KG retrieval) can use drift-immune structure-grounded negative mining. Code is the ideal testbed due to formal semantic guarantees.\\n\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"On SWE-bench Lite, StructFuse with data-flow-aware hard negatives achieves X% recall@5 while embedding-distance negatives achieve Y% and random negatives Z%, demonstrating that structure-grounded negative mining provides complementary training signal not captured by text-based methods alone.\\\"\\n\\n## Dual-Pillar Motivation (per Human Review)\\n\\n1. **Primary — Complementary signal dimension**: Structural connectivity encodes a relevance dimension entirely absent from textual signals. No amount of text-based training can recover inter-procedural data-flow relationships from token sequences alone.\\n2. **Secondary — Selection drift immunity**: Structural graph is externally fixed; negative set does not drift with encoder updates (unlike embedding-distance negatives which shift every epoch).\\n\\n## Formal Definition\\n\\nGiven retrieval domain D, structural graph G, similarity function s, query q:\\n**structure-grounded hard negative** = argmax_{x ∈ D : s(x,q) > τ ∧ ¬reachable_G(x,q)} s(x,q)\\n\\nThe structural graph is externally fixed and does not update with the encoder.\\n\\n## Title\\n\\n\\\"StructFuse: Structure-Grounded Hard Negative Mining for Contrastive Code Context Retrieval\\\"\\n(Per Human Review: emphasize methodology, de-emphasize SE-specific framing)\\n\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\n\\n1. **EMNLP vs SE**: Frame as contrastive learning methodology with code as instantiation\\n2. **Domain-specific concern**: Cross-domain generalization in Discussion (molecular networks, circuit topology, KG retrieval)\\n3. **Incremental contribution**: Novelty is in WHAT generates negatives (program analysis), not the objective itself\\n4. **CodeQL dependency**: CodeQL is one instantiation; any structural analysis producing a connectivity graph works\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n| Concern | Response | Evidence |\\n|---------|----------|----------|\\n| Too domain-specific | Cross-domain generalization + formal definition is domain-agnostic | Discussion section |\\n| Why not ARISE? | Intra-procedural only + inference-time only; we cross call boundaries + train | Direct comparison in ablation |\\n| CodeQL scalability | 0% timeout at pilot, 4% at 50-repo scale; scope-bounded 2-hop | P1 + scale check |\\n| Small improvement | Training methodology contribution, not benchmark chasing; improvement ceiling analysis | Failure analysis |\\n| connected⊆gold? | Scale effect: 40.8% instances have connected≠gold at 50-repo scale | M1 full statistics |\\n| Single encoder (Dream) | Add StarCoder-3B or CodeBERT validation; even 1K-instance experiment suffices | plan_013 |\\n| Static vs dynamic mining (Dream) | Clarify mining is static; add dynamic mining baseline row | plan_015 |\\n| Temperature regime (Dream) | Ablate τ ∈ {0.05, 0.1, 0.2} to confirm finding is not regime-specific | plan_014 |\\n| Missing related work (Dream) | RepoFusion, GRACE, GraphCodeBERT, ANCE, curriculum HN mining | Writing task |\\n| No inference-time graph comparison (Dream) | Discuss relationship to CodexGraph/LocAgent — complementary vs alternative | plan_017 |\\n| SWE-bench stat significance (Dream) | Bootstrap 95% CI for resolve rate | plan_016 |\\n| \\\"Complementary\\\" overstatement (Dream) | Temper abstract: \\\"enhances text-based retrieval\\\" not \\\"cannot recover\\\" | Writing task |\\n\\n## Contingency Framing (from Dream, three-tier thresholds per D009)\\n\\n### Z% ≥ 35%: Best framing holds\\n- Claim 1 as headline, full story intact\\n- Target: EMNLP Main\\n\\n### 20% ≤ Z% < 35%: Median pivot\\n- **Headline shifts to Claim 2** (\\\"embedding-distance negatives harm\\\") which is robust across all tiers\\n- Claim 1 downgrades to \\\"dataflow negatives are a viable, non-harmful alternative\\\"\\n- Title pivot: \\\"When Hard Negatives Hurt: Embedding-Distance Negative Mining Degrades Contrastive Code Retrieval\\\"\\n- Target shifts from EMNLP Main to Findings\\n\\n### Z% < 20%: Major adjustment required\\n- Claim 1 removed from paper or reframed as \\\"when Z% is sufficient, structural negatives help\\\" (conditional claim with narrow scope)\\n- Paper becomes empirical analysis: \\\"Why Structure-Grounded Hard Negatives Don't Help Code Retrieval (Yet)\\\"\\n- Claim 2 (embedding harm) + Claim 3 (scalability analysis) + failure taxonomy carry the paper\\n- Target: EMNLP Findings or workshop (NLP4SE, ML4Code)\\n- **Decision point**: if Z% < 15%, discuss with user whether to continue or pivot project\\n\\n## MVP Evidence Summary\\n\\n- P1: CodeQL feasible (timeout 0%, Fisher p=1.54e-7, instance recall 75%)\\n- P2a: DataFlow feature AUC=0.78, BM25 coefficient negative (-1.80)\\n- P2b: random >> embedding_distance (-27pp R@1 at pool=26, 3 seeds consistent); dataflow untestable at MVP scale but viable at full scale (Z=40.8%). Note: pool=50 eval exists but confounded by val.jsonl distribution shift; use pool=26 data only until M2 clean eval.\\n\", \"total_chars\": 7310, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

structural negatives help\\\" (conditional claim with narrow scope)\\n- Paper becomes empirical analysis: \\\"Why Structure-Grounded Hard Negatives Don't Help Code Retrieval (Yet)\\\"\\n- Claim 2 (embedding harm) + Claim 3 (scalability analysis) + failure taxonomy carry the paper\\n- Target: EMNLP Findings or workshop (NLP4SE, ML4Code)\\n- **Decision point**: if Z% < 15%, discuss with user whether to continue or pivot project\\n\\n## MVP Evidence Summary\\n\\n- P1: CodeQL feasible (timeout 0%, Fisher p=1.54e-7, instance recall 75%)\\n- P2a: DataFlow feature AUC=0.78, BM25 coefficient negative (-1.80)\\n- P2b: random >> embedding_distance (-27pp R@1 at pool=26, 3 seeds consistent); dataflow untestable at MVP scale but viable at full scale (Z=40.8%). Note: pool=50 eval exists but confounded by val.jsonl distribution shift; use pool=26 data only until M2 clean eval.\\n\", \"total_chars\": 7310, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"

exp_id: mvp_p2b_contrastive_training\\n    role: trigger\\n    result: dataflow≡embedding 因 connected⊆gold\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D006\\n  - D004\\n- id: D008\\n  date: 2026-05-12 20:38 UTC\\n  summary: MVP 条件性通过：P1+P2a PASS，P2b dataflow 在 MVP 规模不可测但 50-repo 规模验证确认方法可行（Z=40.8%）。进入\\n    research 阶段。\\n  rationale: MVP 三阶段结果：P1 PASS（timeout 0%, Fisher p=1.54e-7, 75% instance recall）；P2a\\n    PASS（AUC 0.78, BM25 系数为负）；P2b 部分有效——random>>embedding（-27pp recall@1 at pool=26,\\n    -3.7pp at pool=50）确认 naive hard negatives 有害，但 dataflow vs random 因 connected⊆gold\\n    无法在 8 repos 上区分。50-repo 规模验证 Z=40.8%（中型 repo 55.7%），timeout 4%，确认方法在全量数据上可行。Kill\\n    criteria 均未触发。P2b dataflow 对比将在 M2 全量训练中完成。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Key findings:

1. **M1 extraction is actively running** — logs show entries up to 09:40:39 (just now). Good, it's alive and processing.

2. **GPU 4 is idle** — no compute apps on GPU 4. The ROADMAP says "M2 early pilot 启动中" but cuda:4 has 0% utilization. This could mean:
   - The M2 pilot hasn't actually started yet (Agent is still preparing)
   - It started and finished (unlikely, just approved at 01:38)
   - It's being set up but not launched yet
   
3. **GPU 2 is running a separate workload** — PID 55024 with VLLM::EngineCore consuming 22114 MiB, which belongs to a different project. Our assignment to cuda:4 means we're not crossing any GPU boundaries, and M1's CodeQL extraction is purely CPU-bound anyway.

4. **The ROADMAP metrics look promising** — Z% at 74.0% is well above the 35% threshold for Best Framing, though we're still early at 24 out of 222 repos processed.  The 5128 instances sampled give us decent statistical confidence, and the trend from the initial 50-repo check (Z=40.8%) shows positive momentum as we process the denser SWE-Smith dataset.

5. **M2 pilot status needs clarification** — using idle cuda:4 while M1 runs CPU-only is efficient, but the ROADMAP's "正在执行" status is premature since cuda:4 hasn't actually started processing yet.

6. **Coverage matrix shows a tracking gap** — the registry experiments are complete but disconnected from the plan entries, so the 0/17 coverage is really a bookkeeping issue rather than actual missing work.

7. **Critical path timeline** — M1 finishes around May 15 with ~46h remaining, then M2 full (30h), plan_013 (15h), and M3a (40h) run sequentially on GPU, landing us at May 18.5. That gives roughly 6.5 days before the May 25 deadline to handle P1 work requiring 90h of GPU time. The M2 early pilot can actually start now on the existing 5K dataset while M1 continues.

8. **GPU capacity check** — 185h total needed fits within the 288h available on cuda:4 over 12 days, so we're good on budget.

9. **Two loose ends** — the ROADMAP claims M2 pilot is "正在执行" but cuda:4 appears idle, which suggests either the job hasn't actually started yet or the ROADMAP is ahead of what's actually running. Also need to circle back on what M3a specifically requires. The M2 early pilot using 5K instances with 3 strategies on cuda:4 should give directional signal on whether the dataflow approach outperforms random at scale. I'm noticing a bookkeeping gap though—the experiment plan shows M1 as "planned" when it's actually running, and there's no registry entry for the M2 pilot that's supposedly starting. Overall the ROADMAP update looks solid with Z%=74% supporting the best framing and M2 making good use of idle GPU time, though I should flag that the status inconsistencies need cleanup. I'm recalibrating which issues warrant warnings versus info-level flags—the M2 pilot not actually running despite the roadmap claim is the real concern since it means GPU resources are sitting idle, and the coverage matrix gap is equally critical because it breaks our ability to track progress reliably.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Critical finding! The M2 pilot has FAILED with an OSError — can't connect to huggingface.co to load the tokenizer. This is a known AutoDL issue — the `source /etc/network_turbo` proxy may not be working, or the model isn't cached locally.

The script does `source /etc/network_turbo 2>/dev/null || true` but the error shows it couldn't connect to HuggingFace. The model (likely Qwen2.5-Coder-3B) needs to be locally cached or the network issue needs fixing.

Wait — the script sets WANDB_MODE=offline and HF_HUB_DISABLE_XET=1, but the model itself needs to be cached. 

The error confirms the model isn't in the HF cache at `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache` — it should be there from MVP training, unless the disk cleanup wiped it. Either way, this is a blocker that needs to be escalated to the Director.

The training data files are all present and correctly sized, so the data prep succeeded. The failure happened when the training script tried to load the model for the random strategy. Let me check what model name the script is actually using.
```

</details>

2. **Model IS cached**: `models--Qwen--Qwen2.5-Coder-3B` exists in the HF cache at `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/hub/`.

4. The network proxy activated (showing "设置成功"), but the HuggingFace connection still timed out due to AutoDL proxy instability.

**Key points to verify:**

1. **Migration strategy**: 
   - M1 extraction stays on cqa1-21977 (CPU-only, makes sense)
   - M2 early pilot finishes on cqa1-21977 cuda:4
   - All future GPU work goes to bjb1-42106
   - This approach avoids interrupting running processes

2. **GPU upgrade benefits**:
   - 96GB VRAM >> 24GB — M3a (Qwen3.6-27B) no longer needs fallback (7B/quantized)
   - 2 GPUs allows parallel M2 full + plan_013
   - This addresses the M3a VRAM concern I flagged earlier

3. **New GPU allocation**: cuda:0 + cuda:1 on bjb1-42106
   - ROADMAP says this is the new allocation
   - Need to verify the project config has been updated

4. **Risks**:
   - Data migration: M

1 output needs to be transferred from cqa1-21977 to bjb1-42106
   - Environment setup: new server needs all dependencies installed
   - Code sync: structfuse repo needs to be on the new server for plan_013

5. **ROADMAP mentions**: There's a note about embedding_distance actually using BM25 instead of true embedding mining — I need to verify if this is something I found or if it's from the reviewer. Looking at the p2b_5reruns results, dataflow_aware and embedding_distance are performing nearly identically to 4 decimal places, which suggests I should investigate this discrepancy further.

Let me check the server status, verify that M1 extraction and M2 early pilot are still running on cqa1-21977, and investigate whether the embedding_distance strategy is actually using BM25 scores rather than real embedding distances.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Key findings:

1. **bjb1-42106 GPU 0**: 100% util, 0 MiB, but no compute apps listed. This is likely a driver initialization issue or a monitoring artifact. Not a concern — no actual process is using it.

2. **M2 early pilot on cqa1-21977**: Progressing well! 
   - Currently at step 1300, entering Epoch 2
   - eval@1000: val_loss=0.549, recall@1=0.528
   - This is the random strategy (first of 3)
   - recall@1=0.528 at step 1000 seems reasonable for an early pilot on 5K instances
   
3. **M1 extraction**: Still actively running with recent timestamps, continuing to process instances.

Looking at the D013 migration strategy, the hardware upgrade is compelling — doubling VRAM to 96GB per GPU and adding a second unit removes the need for fallbacks and opens up parallel workload capacity. The approach of letting current jobs finish before migrating makes sense operationally, though I should verify that GPU 0 issue on bjb1-42106 isn't masking something deeper before committing to the migration.

The config update is critical — I need to confirm the project settings reflect the new cuda:0 and cuda:1 allocation rather than pointing to the old cqa1-21977 cuda:4. The M1 output and M2 training data both need to be transferred over as well.

There's something important in the ROADMAP notes about the embedding_distance strategy actually using BM25 under the hood. If that's true, then the claim about embedding-distance negatives harming retrieval might actually be about BM25-distance negatives instead, which would significantly change what we're testing.

Looking at the p2b_5reruns registry though, there's a warning that dataflow_aware and embedding_distance produce nearly identical results across all seeds — likely a code bug where they're using the same negative sampling logic. The ROADMAP explicitly mentions needing to implement true embedding-based negative mining before the full M2 run, which suggests the current implementation isn't actually using the encoder's embeddings to find hard negatives.

So the methodological issue is clear: if "embedding_distance" doesn't actually use embedding distances, then the conclusion about embedding negatives being harmful is misleading. I need to check the actual training data preparation code to see what these strategies are really doing.
```
